### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 13 日

2 群益投顧 
  公司 ESG 政策：  
(一 )企業對於因應氣候變遷或溫室氣體管理之策略  
1.萬海秉持對環境保護的承諾，除符合 IMO 國際公約規範之下，進行船隊
減排，減少空汙，並且落實  設備保養及改善，確保機器良好運作，以達達
到環保之要求，愛護地球，永續經營。  2. 萬海積極參與國際環保平台，透
過實際行動來推行節能減碳的環保策略：  (1)2015 年參與 Clean Cargo 
Working Group (CCWG) 組織，加強與同業分享環保知識、資訊、技  術的交
流，以推動節能減碳為目的。自 2017 年起依據 CCWG 新規範，萬海提供
CCWG 的船隊相關航  行、油耗數據須由驗證單位檢驗，萬海船隊已連續五
年 (2016 ~ 2021 年度 )排放數據均通過第三方  驗證單位 DNV 之驗證，此將
可主動吸引同為其會員的貨主、承攬業選擇使用符合其環保政策之航商。
(2) 持續參與世界航運協會 (World Shipping Council,WSC) ，針對各項航運事
務及環境保護議題，  進行討論。萬海為該協會會員，副董事長為該協會之
理事會成員。  (3)2019 年加入零排放聯盟 (Getting to ZeroCoalition) ，萬海
持續推動並實現國際航運減少碳  排放為宗旨，致力達成該聯盟目標。  
(二 )企業溫室氣體排放量減量目標  
依照 IMO 規定，以 2008 年為基準降低碳排率，  以 2021 年為止減少 30%
碳排放率，並預計於 2030 年可  以達到 50% 減排率目標，優於 IMO 要求；
未來將持續進行船隊汰舊換新計畫，並配以新型節能主機及  各式最新節能
設備，以達節能減碳、環境保護目標。  
(三 )企業溫室氣體排放量減量之預算與計畫  
1.預算：保護地球本為每一個企業之責任，本公司將以提升船舶性能，降低
船隊油耗，使用低硫燃  油，以降低溫室氣體排放，視該成本為必要性支出。
2. 船舶能源效率管理計畫 (Ship Energy Efficiency Management Plan
(SEEMP) ：  依國際海事組織 (IMO) 之船舶能源效率管理計畫 (Ship Energy 
Efficiency ManagementPlan, SEEMP) 規範訂定有效的能源效率管理制度，並
根據能源效率設計指標 (EnergyEfficient Design Index, EEDI) 作為新船能源
效率的標準，並據實揭露能源效率營運指標 (Energy Efficient Operational
Index, EEOI) 。  3. 萬海另依 IMO 法規要求制定 SEEMP PART II ，加入「燃
油消耗數據收集計畫」 (DataCollection Plan, DCP) ，藉由執行燃油消耗數據
的監測，管理和報告船舶於海上航行時所產生的二氧化碳排  放，並依法規
製作年度排放報告，經第三方查核認證後提交給國際海事組織。  
4Q26 近洋線進入需求旺季：  
3Q26 以來遠洋線走勢呈現分化。受惠中國十一長假前的拉貨需求，颱風因
素導致港口作業效率降低，加上巴拿馬運河限制通行船舶艘數、船舶周轉率
下滑等因素疊加影響之下，支撐美國線運價持續走高；歐洲線運價走勢相對
疲弱，主因需求逐漸進入旺季尾聲，加上部分航線復航紅海、潛在運力增加
所致。  
美國線方面， 10/2026 需求仍相對有撐，隨後將進入傳統需求淡季，而農曆
春節前拉貨需求有望再度回溫。巴拿馬運河先前受到降雨不足影響而緊縮
通航條件， 10/15/2026 開始每日通航預約名額放寬至 33 艘 (正常為 36 艘 )，

近洋線方面， 4Q26 進入傳統需求旺季，運價有望延續 3Q26 漲勢。

燃油價格處於高檔震盪：  
油價方面，近期新加坡低硫油價格持續回落。美伊戰爭前約 500 美元 /噸，
戰爭後一度上漲至 1,120 美元 /噸，近期則來到近 890 美元 /噸。以目前運價
水準而言，已完全轉嫁燃油價格變動對營運成本造成的影響。  
新船下水規劃：  
公司至 2025 年底船隊達 116 艘，運力共計 57.27 萬 TEU。 2026 年預計交付
6 艘船舶，包括 2 艘 7,000TEU 及 4 艘 8,700TEU 船舶， 2027 年預計交付 1
艘 8,700TEU 及 10 艘 16,000TEU 船舶。  
營收比重方面， 2025 年美國線比重約 23% 、中東印度線比重約 27% 、亞洲
近洋線比重約 37% 、南美線比重約 13% 。

1Q26 處於需求淡季，加上市場預期紅海復航導致運價低迷，萬海 1Q26 營
收 336.41 億元，毛利率由 4Q25 的 23.92% 下滑至 22.76% ，營業利益 QoQ-
7.86% ，另業外認列金融資產評價利益，單季稅後獲利 76.72 億元，稅後 EPS
2.73 元。  
2Q26 受惠於需求回升、運價調漲，營收 QoQ+27.52% ，毛利率 30.37% ，營
業利益 QoQ+91.12% ；業外認列金融資產評價利益之下，單季稅後獲利
115.35 億元，稅後 EPS 4.11 元。  
3Q26 單季營收 QoQ+38.96% ，主因遠洋線為傳統需求旺季，適逢氣候異常
導致亞洲港口塞港、以及巴拿馬運河限航等因素疊加影響下，支撐美國線運
價持續走高，近洋線同樣呈現上漲趨勢所致。預估單季稅後獲利 229.78 億
元，稅後 EPS 8.19 元。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌，亦將出具降評報告。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 187: 9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象

Source PDF: all report pdf/9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日(水)

1/3 
9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象  
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・時事通信は10月６日付で「日銀が『基調的な物価上昇率』について、２％程度に到達したと
判断する方向で検討に入ることが５日、分かった」と報じた。記事内には10月29・30日開催
の金融政策決定会合で取りまとめる展望レポートで、そうした認識が記述されるとしてい
る。

・筆者はこの報道が現実になる可能性が高いと判断している。日銀は予てから基調的物価上昇
率が２％に到達する時期を「2026年度後半から2027年度」と予想してきたので（2026年度後
半にあたる）10月にそうした認識を示さないと、ハト派的な印象を与えてしまうことにな
る。

・既に日銀の政策態度は基調的物価上昇率を２％に「引き上げる」段階から、２％以上に「上
振れさせない」段階に移行している。局面が変化した理由としては、やはり原油高とそれに
対する企業の価格設定行動の積極化が大きいだろう。従来、原油高は企業収益を圧迫するこ
とで、賃上げを阻害し、個人消費の停滞に繋がってきた。そうした環境下で内需は拡大しな
いため、原油高は短期的にインフレ要因となっても、やや長い目でみるとデフレ的な色彩を
帯びていた。もっとも、2026年春先以降の原油高局面では、政府の手厚い支援もあり、企業
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12 月に 1.5％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/3 
は価格転嫁を積極的に進めた。企業物価指数は４月に前月比＋2.8％と即座に上昇し、前年比
伸び率は８月時点でも＋7.6％と高止まりしている。日銀短観（６月および９月調査）では１
年後の販売価格見通しが急伸した。こうした早期かつ大幅な価格転嫁の進展によって、企業
収益は傷みにくくなっている。このことは、名目賃金と名目消費が伸びやすい構図にあるこ
とを浮き彫りにしている。

・日銀が、基調的物価上昇率が２％に到達したとの認識を示すのであれば、それは利上げペー
スの加速を自ら正当化することになる。さすがに10月の利上げはビハインド・ザ・カーブに
陥ったことを認めたと市場参加者に受け止められる恐れがあるため、見送られると予想する
が、（前回利上げから約３ヶ月が経過する）12月会合では利上げが決定されるだろう。そう
なれば、３～４ヶ月に一度の利上げが標準ペースとなることが市場参加者に広く意識される
だろう。

・利上げを巡っては、経済指標に加えて「政府との距離感」を考慮する必要があり、予想は難
儀であった。しかしながら、ここ数週間で情勢は変化したように感じる。９月25日に片山さ
つき財務相が「官邸とも相談の上、私から初めて申し上げるが、先日の日米首脳会談では、
為替について、トランプ大統領から円安への懸念が示された」と発言したことを皮切りに、
同日には城内経済再生相が「アベノミクス的な意味でのリフレーション政策は、もうそうい
う局面は終わっている」とした。また10月５日に公表された９月30日の経済財政諮問会議の
議事要旨では、民間議員である若田部昌澄氏が「デフレではない状況になりつつある現状で
リフレ政策が終焉を迎えているというのは当然のことだ」との見解を示し、さらに９月の金
融政策決定会合で反対票を投じた佐藤綾野日銀政策委員は６日までに共同通信の単独インタ

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
ビューに応じて「（政策金利を）段階的に調整していく方針には賛成している」とした。９
月18日会合における「主な意見」で内閣府の出席者（おそらく城内大臣）が利上げに消極的
と読める発言をしていたことと整合しないが、それは9月18日から25日の間に政府の態度が変
化した可能性が指摘できる。

・10月会合までに円安が進む、あるいは原油高等の要因によってＦｅｄが利上げに踏み切る事
態となれば、日銀も追随する可能性があるものの、そうした状況に至らなければ、10月は
「既往の利上げ効果を見極めたい」などとして利上げを見送るだろう。

・なお、これらに鑑みて、筆者は2027年７月の審議委員人事が中立ないしはタカ派的な人選に
なると予想する。高田委員と田村委員に考え方の近い人物に白羽の矢が立ち、リフレ派が送
り込まれることはないと考えている。利上げに積極的な２名の後任人事にリフレ派と目され
る人物が浮上した場合、金融市場では長期金利上昇や円安の反応が予想される。

## Document 327: GFHK - Oct Edition

Source PDF: all report pdf/GFHK - Oct Edition.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 30
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Equity Research 
Sector Report | Technology｜Oct 15, 2026 
Oct Edition: Choppy Markets, Intact AI

Overseas  
Technology

Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk 
Henry Huang 
SFC CE No. BYA749 
henryhuang@gfgroup.com.hk

Market to remain choppy: Tech equities were volatile in the past month, with rotation 
toward hyperscaler and internet names. That said , s emi and memory (Korea) 
weakness reflected lower risk appetite due to rising bond yields, AI monetization/ARR 
visibility and L-T AI debates (i.e., Broadcom CEO warned that capital may now be a 
bigger constraint), even as company-specific catalysts continued to be strong. On the 
other hand, the positive was the acceleration of AI model scaling (i.e., Meta’s Muse 
Wonder, Gemini 4, etc). That said, w e view the pullback primarily as a reset in 
expectations rather than the evidence of AI deterioration. Despite light positioning, we 
expect the markets will remain choppy in the near term but would  accumulate low 
valuation & sustainable names  (i.e., HBM, non-TSMC foundries, CPUs, CPOs, etc) . 
In our preferred list, we added CLS, while removing MTK and Hon Hai.  
U.S. roadshow takeaways (week of Oct 5): Investor tone skewed neutral to slightly 
cautious, driven by conservative top ‑down macro views, U.S. election, and ongoing 
debates around the L-T AI demand. Positioning in AI infrastructure has come off peak 
levels. Even  so, many investors would accumulate on pullbacks, with interest 
concentrated in “Made in U.S.” names (INTC, LITE, COHR), U.S. memory suppliers, 
TSMC, and selective bottleneck themes — including ASE, ABF substrates, 
MRVL/SMTC, and Japanese MLCCs. 
OCP & earnings season in focus: The week is anchored by AI/semis — SEMICON 
West, OCP, and earnings from ASML, TSMC, etc. OCP reinforced the industry’s pivot 
toward rack‑scale infrastructure, with inference and agentic workloads driving demand 
across compute, memory, and networking. Scale‑up optics, NPO/CPO, and 800VDC 
power were key themes, while AMD’s CPU outlook and Helios/Helion underscored a 
secular CPU uptrend. Nvidia ’s use of PTFE for NVLink boards further supports our 
positive stance on PTFE (see PCB Spec in 2 H27, Oct 5).  On earnings, ASML 
delivered largely in -line results, GM and OP above consensus with revenue slightly 
miss, and raised full year guidance again and is exploring ways to increase capacity 
by >30% 2028 to fulfill strong customer demand, also extending the order visibility 
beyond 2028. For TSMC, we anticipate an inline quarter. 
September monthly sales/data: TSMC sales -0.6% MoM, slightly ahead. Rubin to 
accelerate and T3 ramp. PC forecast trimmed. Substrate/CCL upcycle with 
pricing/margin expansion. Aspeed revenue +101% YoY. Details on page 5. 
GFHK Tech Most Read:  1) Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9 ; 2) Michelle Jing 
on PCB Spec in 2H27; 3) Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration; 4) Jeff Pu on NAND Re-
acceleration; 5) Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview.

Sector Report | Technology

GFHK Tech Important Stock Calls  
In the past one month, our key reports and stock calls are: 
Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9, reported date on Oct 13: We expect near-term MTK headwinds 
from TPU v9 delays but remain constructive on MTK/MRVL longer term. Humufish’s redesign could 
push tape-out to Jan -27, MP tape -out to 2Q27 and shipments to 1Q28, while EMIB -T yields could 
recover from 45–50%. Rising TPU allocations to Anthropic could constrain Gemini compute, though 
higher internal allocation in 2H26 may support future models. SemiAnalysis expects Anthropic/OpenAI 
to each add 5 –10GW in 2027 vs. a few GW for Google. For TPU v10 (4Q28 /1Q29), MTK/GUC’s 
Icefish remains the only confirmed mainstream SKU, while Broadcom-led Merope faces AMD/Marvell 
competition, with both potentially testing EMIB-T. 
Michelle Jing on PCB Spec in 2H27, reported date on Oct 5: Some market watchers expect Nvidia 
to shift from PTFE to hydrocarbon resin (HC) for the VR300 NVL576 switch tray. HC—one of the glass 
free alternatives within PTFE hybrid architectures—has only recently passed SI testing and has yet to 
enter PCB MP, leaving it roughly three months behind PTFE in qualification. On the contrary, we 
believe that PTFE offers superior performance for high frequency, high speed designs. With VR300 
NVL576 shipments expected in 2H27 and the final configuration likely set by end 26 —and with a 
leading Chinese PCB ma ker already achieving meaningful PTFE hybrid yields —we maintain that 
PTFE hybrid remains the most probable solution for next year’s switch board. 
Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration , reported date on  Sept 29 : Nvidia’s CPO scale -out switch is 
progressing faster than expected, with FAU yields improving. We forecast CPO switch shipments at 
15k/16k/30k in 4Q26/1Q27/2Q27 and 100k in 2027. For Nvidia/AWS scale -up, CPO and NPO 
continue in parallel, with full NPO adoption expected at NVL576 launch. Feynman is also devel oping 
both variants, with CPO potentially using QM6 + six 6.4T OEs. We shift AWS Trainium 4 launch to 
1Q28 and forecast global scale-up OE demand at 6.5m/42m in 2027E/28E. 
Jeff Pu on NAND Re -acceleration, reported date on Sept 20 : According to TrendForce, NAND 
demand is stronger than expected as U.S. CSPs scale AI infrastructure, with eSSD demand nearly 
2x prior forecasts. We raise NAND contract price growth to +21%/+17%/+15% QoQ in 
3Q26/4Q26/1Q27, while hyperscaler inventories decline. Supply remains disci plined at 15%/21%/20% 
YoY bit growth in 2025 –27. Demand is supported by agentic AI, vector databases and KV -cache 
offloading, where eSSD offers lower cost/bit than DRAM and better power efficiency than HDD. 
Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview , reported date on  Oct 7 : TSMC shares hit record highs on 
expectations ahead of the print and potential Terafab collaboration. We expect largely inline 3Q26 
results, capex and full-year guidance, with softer 4Q26 margin (67.2%). Management should reaffirm 
2026 capex at the ceiling and mid-40s YoY growth, while we forecast 37% growth in 2027 on sustained 
AI/CPU/networking demand and price hikes. Key focuses include margins, long -term capex/U.S. 
expansion, large-reticle packaging and A14. We raise 2026E/27E EPS by 2%/8% on hig her N2/N3 
capacity and operating leverage, and roll to 20x 2027E P/E, lifting TP to NT$2,960.

### Page 5

Sector Report | Technology

September Monthly Data  
TSMC September sales of NT$511.9bn, -0.6% MoM and +54.6% YoY, with total 3Q revenue reaching 
NT$1.49T, +17.6 QoQ, slightly ahead of expectations of +14%. The solid performance was well 
supported by ASIC, A20 Pro, and Rubin. For 3Q26, we expect gross margin to be 67.8%, higher than 
guidance of 65-67% and consensus of 66.4% . For 4Q26, we expect another 12% QoQ growth with 
GM at 67.2%, both ahead of street’s estimates of +10% QoQ and 66% margin. Ahead of the earnings 
call, key focus include gross margin trajectory, long -term capex discipline, and the execution of its 
U.S. fab expansion. 
Rubin to accelerate:  Hon Hai revenue last month was NT$1.16T, +26 % MoM, +38% YoY, mainly 
driven by iPhone build.  Quanta and Wistron delivered -23% and -17% MoM respectively, due to AI 
racks’ shipping timing differences. Of note, Pegatron surprised the market with +143.2% MoM revenue 
surge fueled by initial GB300 rack shipments to neocloud customers including Lambda and Together 
AI. This reaffirms our thesis of robust NVL72 order momentum across Neoclouds. Looking ahead, 
VR200 is ramping now, with volume deployment accelerating into 2027. We maintain our forecast of 
75k and 92k NVL72 (GB+VR) rack shipments for 2026 and 2027, respectively. Concurrently, Wiwynn 
delivered another solid month with revenue expanding 20.0% MoM, underpinne d by resilient GP 
server demand alongside the ramp of Trainium 3. 
ASIC ramping up: On design service vendors fronts, driven  by T3 ramp, Alchip’s Sep revenue 
reached a record NT$9.9 8bn. Backed by secured incremental 3nm wafer allocation and improving 
yields, management raised its 2027 revenue guidance, now targeting growth above the previous 50% 
YoY baseline. That said, we push out T4 shipment timeline into 1Q28. GUC delivered a strong beat 
with monthly revenue reaching a record NT$7.145bn (+22.4% MoM, +97.8 % YoY), driven by robust 
Axion 2 CPU demand. Beyond its engagement with top 4 CSPs, multi -year order visibility into 2027 
and beyond is further bolstered by high-margin (likely TK1) automotive ASIC designs. 
PC forecast trimmed: NB shipments from top 5 ODMs were 9.7m in Sep, with 3Q26 shipments down 
17% QoQ and 26% YoY. Global PC OEM shipments contracted 20.1% YoY in 3Q26, worse than 
expected, post robust 1H26 pull -ins with high pricing points and weak macro. For 2027, we project 
the industry will decline a further 5% YoY. On the competitive front, tier -1 vendors such as Lenovo, 
Dell, and HP are not immune to this downturn, while entry-level laptops (low cost models from China) 
continue to see stronger relative demand.  
Substrate/CCL Upcycle Intact: Unimicron continued to deliver another all-time high sales with +5% 
MoM and 65% YoY. Supported by ABF upward pricing of ~15% in 2H26 (likely more next year) and 
higher mix of AI products, we expect a meaningful GM uptick for this quarter with momentum to sustain 
into 2027 . In the meantime, ZengDing’s revenue rose 28% MoM and 36% YoY mainly driven by  
iPhone, 1.6T mSAP, and Rubin’s HDI. On the CCL front, bolstered by ongoing price hikes and strong 
demand from GP servers, ITEC revenue grew 15.5% MoM and 111% YoY, and NanYa Plastics 
recorded +12.5% MoM and +55% YoY. Meanwhile, EMC and TUC both posted another single digit 
growth MoM on sales.

Downstream tech – Smartphone & Apple  
We maintain Global smartphone forecast of -14% in 2026  
According to IDC, the preliminary 2Q26 smartphone data points to a still ‑soft market with uneven 
regional dynamics, and their commentary continues to emphasize structural rather than cyclical 
headwinds. Smartphone shipments remain under pressure from weak replacement cycles, cautious 
consumer spending, and limited breakthrough features. IDC typically highlights that “growth is 
concentrated in the premium tier,” driven by ecosystem lock ‑in, while the mass market remains 
challenged by price sensitivity. They also tend to note that China and parts of emerging markets show 
only modest stabilization, not a full recovery, and that vendors are prioritizing margin discipline over 
volume.  
Overall, by factoring in higher iPhone 17/17 Pro volume, we fine tune our global smartphone shipment 
forecast to -14% YoY from -15% YoY. 
For 2027, we expect global smartphone shipments to be flattish YoY. That said, 2026 -2027 will be 
one of the most challenging periods for the industry, driven primarily by sharply higher memory prices, 
which have already pushed OEMs to reduce shipments and raise device prices.

iPhone 18 Pro/18 PM demand lukewarm  
iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max wait-time lukewarm: iPhone 18 Pro and iPhone 18 Pro Max pre-orders began 
on Sept 12 in the U.S. and many other countries. Generally speaking, the wait-time appears lukewarm, 
with: 1) 3–4 weeks for 256GB/512GB Pro Max across all colors, as well as Burgundy 1TB in the U.S.；
2) 2–3 weeks for Glacier 256GB/512GB Pro Max in China; 3) 1–2 weeks for Black/Glacier 1TB Pro 
Max in the U.S. ；4) immediate availability for Silver 1TB and mos t 2TB Pro Max models in the U.S, 
as well as most iPhone 18 Pro configurations except for Burgundy 256GB in the U.S. We attribute the 
lukewarm wait-time to limited spec upgrade, higher pricing (especially for large storage models), etc. 
We also expect the Pro Max to be impacted by iPhone Duo and the criticism of heavy weight.  
iPhone Duo feedback generally positive 
The iPhone Duo, priced at $1,999 in the U.S., opened pre‑orders on Oct 16 and officially launched on 
Oct 23. Early reviews were positive, highlig hting its polished folding experience, high ‑quality inner 
display, and improved multitasking in iOS 27, but also point to trade ‑offs including a heavier and 
thicker form factor, a visible crease, and a dual ‑camera. Due to hinge constraints, we are reducing  
the iPhone Duo EMS build plan from 7m to 6m units in 2026. We also lower the iPhone 18 Pro/Pro 
Max build plan to 72m units given the constraints associated with the adjustable‑aperture camera.

Downstream tech – PC 
Global PC units down to -12% in 2026, followed by another decline in 2027 
On Oct 8, IDC reported 3Q26 global PC shipments fell 20.1% YoY, missing street expectations of a 
10–15% decline. The sharper contraction was primarily driven by front -loaded demand in 1H26, 
elevated ASPs, and broader macro weakness. This aligns with our prior view that ODM build 
momentum moderated starting in end -2Q26, amid the rising concerns over channel inventory. 
Meanwhile, the top 5 ODMs also saw 3Q26 shipments decline 26% YoY. Looking ahead, we model 
4Q26 shipments to continue 20% YoY drop (flattish QoQ), underperforming normal seasonality. 
On the component front, we expect memory de -spec to normalize. End -users still require sufficient 
DRAM capacity (16GB+) to handle on -device AI workloads, and PC DDR 5 supply constraints have 
eased compared to Server’s. On pricing, new models will still have higher pricing points to pass 
through 2H component cost increases across memory, CPU, etc. That said, we anticipate price hikes 
will remain measured vs 1H26 given sluggish end-market demand. 
Overall, reflecting weaker than expected 3Q26 results post pull -ins and a muted 4Q outlook, we trim 
our 2026 PC shipment forecast to -12% YoY. For 2027, we project shipments to decline further by 5% 
YoY, as higher ASP and persistent macro headwinds continue to weigh on demand.

Upstream tech – AI Semi & Supply Chain  
U.S. CSP capex to be +85%/+53%/+20% in 2026/2027/2028 
Given capex expands further into 2027, CSPs free cash flow are undergoing significant pressure and 
turning negative except Microsoft, such that shifting to debt financing for their massive capex. In 2025, 
Top 5 CSPs’ total debt issuance reached $108bn, or 24% of total capex . Per our Addressing 2028 
Capex and AI Platform Value Shift report on Aug 6, we forecast Top 5 CSPs’ debt issuance to reach 
$257bn/$419bn in 2026/2027, or 31%/35% of total capex.  Assuming debt issuance to grow in 
2028/2029 at a mild pace, we believe U.S. CSPs' balance sheet (except Oracle) to remain very healthy, 
due to net leverage (EBITDA basis) of only 0.5 and the net debt-to-equity ratio of only 18% by 2029.  
Despite healthy balance sheet for most CSPs, we expect most to safeguard balance sheets beyond 
2027 amid widening spreads and FCF profiles, with the focus shifting to returns. Together with a swing 
factor of competition from Chinese LLMs, we expect U.S. CSPs to decelerate capex expansion. We 
forecast Top 5 CSPs capex of $1,512bn/1,739bn in 2028/2029, or +20%/15% YoY, down from +53% 
in 2027.

Server CPU TAM to grow at 49% CAGR by 2030 
As the orchestration core of AI infrastructure buildouts, the Server CPU has gained significant traction 
over the past few months. This is driven by the Agentic AI/inference trend, fueled by the breakthrough 
of OpenClaw and Anthropic’s ARR surging to $44bn  in April. For instance, in latest earnings calls, 
AMD projected a >35% CAGR for Server CPU TAM by 2030, and Intel said the shift toward agentic 
AI is driving a structural demand for CPU, where the GPU-to-CPU ratio is tightening from 8:1 in training 
to 4:1 in inference. Based on our estimates, assuming inference accounts for 90% of AI workloads 
and GPU-to-CPU ratio for AI servers to reach 1:1.2 in 2030, we now expect Server CPU TAM to grow 
96%/71% in 2026/2027, reaching $215bn by 2030 from $30bn in 2025, with a 5-yr CAGR of 49%.

Upstream tech – Optical 
Strong demand for pluggable through 2028. 
We expect that the total demand for 800G/1.6T will reach 90mn/80mn in 2027, increased by 
80%/167%+, mainly driven by: 1) Nvidia, Google and AWS and other ASIC’s Accelerators’ demand 
are ramping up; 2) GPU/ASIC scale -out bandwidth continuously  increase drives increasing 
GPU/ASIC-to-optical module ratio; 3) We expect that TPU for 2027 will be 8.9m and Google fully 
transitions to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up layer will adopt optical interconnects. In 
addition, as we expect that CPO/NPO will start since 2H27, represents a pure incremental opportunity 
for optical interconnects supply chain, benefiting FAU, laser, shuffle box, connector and OE players, 
including LITE, GLW, TSEM, Sumitomo and Browave. We also like Marvell and SMTC, for the 
TIA/Driver used in NPO OE.  
Scale-out bandwidth increase driving optical-module deployment 
For NVIDIA, each Rubin GPU is equipped with two CX9 NIC chips, doubling the scale-out bandwidth 
compared with Blackwell. We further estimate that Rubin Ultra will adopt four CX9 chips per GPU, 
leading to another 2 times increase in scale-out bandwidth, continuing NVIDIA’s consistent bandwidth-
doubling trajectory. Accordingly, we expect the ratio of 1.6T optical modules has risen from 1:2.5 to 
1:5 in Rubin. From CoWoS perspective, we expect 5m/2m Blackwell/Rubin and 20m 1.6T in 2026. 
For Google, we believe that Goo gle fully transition to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up 
layer will adopt optical interconnects. We believe that in the scale-up domain, the ratio of TPU to 1.6T 
optical modules is approximately 1:1.5; In the scale-out domain, the ratio is around 1:2.5, suggesting 
the overall TPU-to-optics ratio is approximately 1:4 (1.6T equivalent).

Nvidia/CSPs’ Scale-out/up CPO/NPO  
Within NVIDIA’s next -gen NVL576 and broader CSPs’ AI infrastructure plans, CPO and NPO are 
developed side -by-side to expand GP U/ASIC interconnect bandwidth: CPO is the ideal long -term 
design for lower power and latency yet faces headwinds from complicated advanced packaging, and 
yield risks, while NPO could deliver most short -reach optical performance gains with far better 
manufacturability and deployment flexibility, making NPO a reliable near -term scaling path and CPO 
the ultimate end -state architecture. NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal 
conditioning and equalization to system designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that 
take over part of traditional DSP functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens of 
dollars worth of combined TIA and driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics. 
Our shipment forecasts at 6.5m/42.4m NPO optical engines in CY27/28.  
Scale-up domain to increase by NVL576 to compensate lower memory: With lower memory spec, we 
expect Nvidia to increase the scale -up domain by optics to enhance performance. According to 
Nvidia’s GTC 2026, th e optical scale -up fabric allows Nvidia to expand Rubin Ultra to an NVL576 
scale-up. Regarding CPO and NPO which are being parallelly developed, we expect NPO to be a 
high reliability scaling path for NPO. We now expect its scale -up to adopt NPO in Rubin U ltra and 
Feynman platform and estimate OE shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028.

### Page 25

Sector Report | Technology

Nvidia’s NPO OE to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028 
For Nvidia, we now expect its scale up to adopt NPO in Rubin Ultra and Feynman platform and 
estimate OE  shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028, based on full NPO 
adoption at NVL576 launch given NPO maturity. Coupled with other CSP’s platform, we estimate total 
OE market to be 6.5m/42.4m in 2027/2028.  
TIA/Driver to benefit from NPO 
NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal conditioning and equalization to system 
designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that take over part of traditional DSP 
functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens o f dollars worth of combined TIA and 
driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics.  
A key architectural shift under NPO is the partial removal of DSP functionality at the module level, with 
signal conditioning and equalization increasing ly redistributed into system -level design. This 
significantly elevates the importance of TIA (transimpedance amplifier) and laser driver components, 
which effectively replace a portion of traditional DSP value. We estimate that each 3.2T optical engine 
will embed high tens of dollars of combined TIA and driver content, representing a meaningful step -
up versus traditional pluggable optics architectures.  
From a competitive standpoint, we expect Marvell to remain the primary beneficiary of both NPO OE 
given i ts stronger positioning high speed TIA/Driver. Semtech is well positioned as a secondary 
beneficiary through its exposure to high-speed analog signal chain components, particularly TIAs and 
laser drivers, which scale directly with optical engine proliferat ion under NPO. We forecast 
6.5mn/42.4mn NPO OE shipments in CY27/28. Based on our estimated front-end content per OE and 
a 30% market share assumption for Semtech, we estimate its NPO revenue of $315/936mn. 
Stock Implication 
Generally speaking, scale-up CPO/NPO is an incremental opportunity for optical interconnect supply 
chain, as it aims to replace copper and has no impact on scale -out side. Additionally, scale up and 
scale out CPO solution share the similar suppliers. That said, we expect the key benefic iaries to be 
components such as FAU, CW laser, ELSFP, shuffle, PIC, TIA and Driver. We: 1) prefer Lumentum/ 
COHR due to the demand upside for CW laser, and also like local cw laser players; 2) expect Browave 
(3163 TT) to benefit, given the sizable contribu tion of CPO’s shuffle box considering ~55% market 
share, $5-6k ASP and a volume ramp in 2H26; 3) we also like TSEM, given CSP preferring NPO 
solution for their ASICs scale up interconnection. 4) expect GLW to benefit, given the second supplier 
of shuffle b ox and main supplier of MMC connector/fiber. 5) Marvell/Semtech will also benefit from 
CSP/Nvidia’s NPO solution, since TIA and driver will upgrade in the NPO Optical Engine.

### Page 28

Sector Report | Technology

Google’s 6D Torus & Beneficiaries.  
We expect Google will migrate its TPU interconnect from 3D to 6D torus, re -organizing the 64 -chip 
elemental block from 4× 4× 4 to 2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2. External optical links per block rise from 96 to 384, with 
optical ports per chip scaling increase from 1:1.5 to 1:6, driving 4x coherent lite transceivers and OCS 
demand. The trajectory aligns with NVIDIA's CPO/NPO move. Both are interconnect scaling that 
expands direct chip-to-chip bandwidth and reduces hop count, with TPU via a 6D torus and NVIDIA 
via CPO/NPO for Scale-up. 
Google’s migration from 3D  to 6D Torus: We expect Google to migrate its TPU interconnect from a 
3D to a 6D torus topology, re-organizing the elemental 64-chip building block from a 4× 4× 4 3D Torus 
cube into a 2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2 hyper-cube. In the current 3D design, each chip connects to 6 nearest 
neighbors across X/Y/Z axes and the cube exposes 16 optical ICI links on each of its 6 faces, totaling 
96 external optical links per cube (~1.5 optical ports per chip). Under the 6D design, each dimension 
has 64 external optical links (2⁶ chips, on e outward link per chip per dimension), for a total of 6 ×  64 
= 384 external optical links (~6 optical ports per chip). The per-chip optical port count thus scales from 
1.5 to 6. Combined with Scale -out at an estimated 1:2.5, this brings the aggregate TPU -to-port ratio 
to 1:8.5. 
Lower latency and closer to non -blocking Scale-up: For a 4,096 -chip superpod, a 3D torus (4× 4× 4) 
carries a worst-case diameter of 24 hops, whereas a 6D torus (2× 2× 2× 2× 2× 2) halves that to 12 hops 
(2× 6), directly compressing collective-operation and latency. Higher dimensionality also spreads traffic 
across 6 orthogonal paths, improving bisection bandwidth and moving closer to non-blocking chip-to-
chip communication. Critically, shortening each edge from 4 to 2 nodes eliminates deep mu lti-hop 
electrical paths inside the block: the architecture deliberately reduces intra -cube copper 
communication and pushes a far larger share of traffic onto optical fiber. 
Key beneficiaries: The exact TPU generation for the adoption of 6D torus and initi al penetration rate 
remain unclear. That said, whether NVIDIA moves toward CPO/NPO or Google expands from 3D to 
6D, both are Scale -up strategies aimed at enabling greater inter -chip communication. Key 
beneficiaries include the 2.4T Coherent Lite module and the OCS supply chain, such as MEMS-based 
optical switch vendors, PIC/laser/DSP suppliers, and fiber/cabling vendors supporting the larger 
reconfigurable lightwave fabric.

Risks 
1) Alternative technologies; 2) AI demand and capex slowdown; 3) IT demand slowdown; 4) product 
delays.

► 美國經濟仍具韌性 ，惟通膨壓力猶存 。觀察
美國9月ISM服務業指數表現 ，內需活動維持
穩健，企業投入成本壓力亦有所升溫；另一
方面，受地緣政治因素影響 ，美國柴油價格
大幅攀升 ，而柴油為卡車運輸 、農業及製造
業的重要燃料 ，若價格持續維持高檔 ，恐進
一步增添通膨壓力 。考量聯準會偏鷹立場 ，
且當前通膨降溫進程不如預期 ，年底前仍保
有升息空間。
► 美國債務總額突破40兆美元，預估2026年公
眾持有聯邦債務占 GDP的比率將達 101%，
財政可持續性風險升溫 。與此同時 ，AI基礎

建設舉債熱潮 ，疊加中東局勢動盪帶來的通
膨不確定性 ，迫使投資人重新定價長天期債
券風險 ，要求更高的風險溢酬 。隨實質殖利
率上行，美債10年期殖利率揚升並持續高懸
於5%水準之上，30年期長債殖利率亦同步站
上近20年來高點 。隨長天期債券利率續揚 ，
殖利率曲線趨陡峭 ，債券配置宜先避開長天
期部位，聚焦中短天期債券鎖利機會。

投資焦點
► 距離美國期中選舉投票僅剩不到1個月，觀察
Polymarket賭盤預估數據顯示 ，眾議院選情
較為明朗，民主黨有超過9成的機率控制眾議
院。但參議院選情則尚有變數 ，今年以來兩
黨均互有領先 ，雖然數據顯示民主黨掌控參
議院機率呈現上揚 ，然隨時序進入選前最後
衝刺期 ，參議院能否亦由民主黨掌控仍待觀
察。故就選前而言 ，由於不同政黨的政策傾
向差異 ，潛在受惠產業亦有不同 ，選舉結果
的不確定性恐令投資人不易大舉押注股市。
► 另一方面 ，油價高檔徘迴 ，導致年底前通 膨
擔憂仍存 ，加上AI企業發債需求成長 、美國
財政體質惡化等 因素共同發酵 ，推動近期 美
債殖利率逐步攀高 ，並令股市預期本益 比壓
力延續 ，進而限縮了投資人短期對美股的追
價動能，短期指數恐維持震盪輪動格局 。可
留意抗波動 、體質佳且具價值優勢之標的 ，
待期中選舉結束與3Q26財報展開後，不確定
性減退將有助資金回歸長線AI題材。
殖利率攀升令美股本益比面臨壓力參議院期中選舉選情仍具變數
期中選前擾動與殖利率走高，美股短期恐仍震盪輪動

投資焦點日股
► 9月日本銀行決議升息 1碼，明確表示潛在
CPI已接近2%，未來也將繼續調高利率 ，政
策重點開始轉向防範通膨向上偏離目標 。然
因有2位委員反對升息，加上總裁植田和男會
後談話偏向中性 ，令市場解讀此次升息未如
預期鷹派，美日利差不易快速縮窄，故9月會
議後日元反而呈現利多出盡的回貶反應。
► 觀察今年以來日股面臨短線日圓升值的狀態
並未出現顯著走弱 態勢。一方面反映企業治
理改革推動股東報酬 提升已成為 支撐股價的
重要結構性因素 。另一方面 ，升息代表日本
經濟已具備一定的健康程度 ，貨幣政策正常
化將不致對需求帶來衝擊 。因此 ，隨BoJ延
續升息方向 ，將擴大銀行淨利差 ，改善其獲
利結構，並提升金融資產收益 。儘管近期整
體日股預期獲利成長走勢略顯停滯 ，然日本
銀行股 在總 體經 濟 與貨幣 政策環 境的 支持
下，獲利成長有望逆勢攀升 ，料將成為後續
資金關注重點。
日圓升值對日股影響似乎有限，關注銀行股獲利走強BoJ升息方向延續，惟其步伐仍取決於經濟與物價

BoJ政策保持正常化路線，日本銀行股有望持續受惠

股市
 公債殖利率走高雖可能壓抑 股債市場近期 動能，然而觀察美股第三季獲利年增 率有望仍為雙位數成
長，或支撐股市表現 。市場持續關注AI資本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場
動盪，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評
價面壓力等紛擾 帶來干擾，可布局防禦 股增強抗震力 。長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且
基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融及醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨 發展。殖利率偏高環境建議以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走
升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸

引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 短期抗波動，逢回可分批布局仍具長線趨勢之AI與科技產業

### Page 15

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Khuyến nghị: MUA – Giá mục tiêu 
25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 đạt 27%, với động lực chính 
đến từ phân khúc KHDN, đặc biệt là từ nhóm FDI trong bối cảnh dòng vốn FDI vào 
Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực. Xét về lĩnh vực, cho vay kinh 
doanh BĐS kỳ vọng sẽ tăng trưởng vượt trội khi ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp 
vốn cho các dự án hạ tầng và dự án BĐS ưu tiên, trong khi các mảng kinh doanh 
khác tăng trưởng ở mức khả quan.

Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM giảm so với năm 2025 do áp lực huy động khiến chi 
phí vốn duy trì ở mặt bằng cao, trong khi lãi suất cho vay dự kiến ở mức cạnh tranh 
để ưu tiên mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng.

Chúng tôi dự phóng tỷ lệ nợ xấu của ngân hàng sẽ tăng 11bps YoY trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất đang neo ở mức cao và kỳ vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì chính sách trích 
lập dự phòng thận trọng nhằm củng cố bộ đệm, qua đó hoàn thành mục tiêu duy 
trì tỷ lệ bao phủ nợ xấu ở mức trên 100%.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các yếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu MBB với giá mục tiêu 
25,950 VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 26.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 17/09/2026.

Cơ cấu tín dụng  Hoạt động kinh doanh

Ngân hàng TMCP Quân đội (MBB) được 
thành lập năm 1994 với định hướng hoạt 
động ban đầu là cung cấp các dịch vụ tài 
chính cho các doanh nghiệp quân đội. Sau 
hơn 30 năm phát triển, MBB hiện đã trở 
thành ngân hàng sở hữu quy mô tài sản và 
tín dụng lớn nhất trong nhóm NH TMCP tư 
nhân. Phát triển theo mô hình tập đoàn, 
MBB đi đầu trong lĩnh vực chuyển đổi số với 
số lượng khách hàng và tỷ lệ CASA đầu 
ngành. 
Nguồn: MBB, KBSV

mức vượt trội so với ngành.

HOẠT ĐỘNG KINH DOANH 
Kết quả kinh doanh tích cực nhờ 
tăng trưởng thu nhập lãi thuần 
 Tín dụng trong 2Q2026 tăng mạnh so với quý trước, đạt 13.2% YTD cùng sự 
phục hồi của NIM đã giúp ngân hàng thiết lập kỷ lục mới về thu nhập lãi thuần 
với 16,894 tỷ đồng (+36.5% YoY). Thu nhập ngoài lãi tiếp tục thu hẹp trong quý 
này do hoạt động kinh doanh ngoại hối và chứng khoán kém tích cực, qua đó 
phần nào làm giảm tốc tăng trưởng tổng thu nhập hoạt động (+18.5% YoY). Chi 
phí dự phòng rủi ro đạt 4,247 tỷ đồng (-11.3% YoY), qua đó giúp LNTT tăng tích 
cực (+40.7% YoY), đạt 10,560 tỷ đồng. Lũy kế 1H2026, LNTT của ngân hàng đạt 
20,188 tỷ đồng, hoàn thành 51% kế hoạch BLĐ đề ra đầu năm.

Tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 được dự 
báo khả quan so với cùng kỳ  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự báo tăng trưởng tín dụng cả năm 2026 ở mức 27%, vượt 
trội hơn mặt bằng chung của ngành nhờ lợi thế tham gia nhận CGBB. Cụ thể: 
− Xét về phân khúc khách hàng, động lực chính kỳ vọng sẽ đến từ phân 
khúc KHDN, trong đó nổi bật là nhóm FDI khi ngân hàng đã chủ động 
thành lập khối Khách hàng FDI chuyên biệt trong bối cảnh dòng vốn 
FDI vào Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực.  
− Xét về lĩnh vực, chúng tôi cho rằng cho vay kinh doanh BĐS sẽ tăng 
trưởng vượt trội với kỳ vọng ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp các dự án 
hạ tầng và dự án BĐS ưu tiên (BĐS KCN, NOXH). Ngân hàng cho biết tỷ 
trọng dư nợ mảng này dự kiến sẽ được duy trì trong biên độ mục tiêu 
13±2% tổng dư nợ tín dụng. Chúng tôi nghiêng về khả năng ngân hàng 
sẽ bám sát cận trên của mục tiêu (15%), nhằm tận dụng dư địa tăng 
trưởng từ các dự án ưu tiên, đồng thời duy trì kiểm soát chất lượng tài 
sản an toàn. Trong khi đó, dư nợ cho vay các mảng khác dự kiến sẽ ở 
mức thấp hơn tăng trưởng chung do môi trường vĩ mô còn nhiều khó 
khăn.

Biểu đồ 8. Cơ cấu tín dụng 
Tỷ trọng cho vay kinh doanh BĐS liên tục gia tăng 
trong các quý gần đây, trong khi cơ cấu cho vay 
thương mại và chế biến chế tạo có xu hướng giảm. 
Tính tới hết 1H2026, tăng trưởng cho vay kinh doanh 
BĐS, thương mại, và chế biến chế tạo lần lượt đạt 
38.2%, 4.9%, và 9.3% YTD.

Nguồn: MBB, KBSV

NIM chịu áp lực giảm so với 2025  Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM cả năm sẽ giảm so với 2025 (-15bps YoY) dựa 
trên luận điểm:  
(1) Ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục dịch chuyển cơ cấu huy động sang các kênh có 
chi phí vốn (COF) cao hơn nhằm đáp ứng mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín 
dụng cao, trong bối cảnh cạnh tranh huy động trên thị trường 1 vẫn 
diễn ra gay gắt. Bên cạnh đó, ngân hàng cho biết dự kiến sẽ phát hành 
10-15 nghìn tỷ trái phiếu tier 2 trong 2H2026 với COF tương đối cao. 
(2) Với mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng cao, chúng tôi cho rằng ngân hàng 
sẽ duy trì lãi suất cho vay ở mức cạnh tranh so với mặt bằng chung của 
ngành. Các khoản vay dự kiến vẫn sẽ tiếp tục tái định giá theo mặt 
bằng lãi suất hiện tại, tuy nhiên tốc độ sẽ chậm lại so với quý 2, do đó 
lợi suất IEA dự kiến sẽ tăng chậm hơn COF trong 2H2026.

Chất lượng tài sản suy giảm trong 
tầm kiểm soát  
 Trong 2Q2026, tỷ lệ hình thành nợ xấu ở mức 1.2% (-16bps QoQ), tương ứng 
4,142 tỷ đồng nợ xấu phát sinh mới, và tỷ lệ nợ xấu tăng nhẹ 3bps so với quý 
trước. Nợ xấu tăng trong quý này chủ yếu tới từ phân khúc bán lẻ (do hoạt động 
kinh doanh và giá đất của khách hàng tại địa phương bị ảnh hưởng sau khi sáp

Định giá – Khuyến nghị: MUA – Giá 
mục tiêu 25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá là P/B và Chiết khấu lợi nhuận thặng 
dư để tìm ra mức giá hợp lý cho cổ phiếu MBB. 
(1) Phương pháp định giá P/B 
Chúng tôi điều chỉnh P/B mục tiêu xuống mức 1.5x trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất neo cao và thanh khoản ngân hàng còn nhiều khó khăn. Dù vậy, 
P/B mục tiêu vẫn cao hơn so với P/B trung bình 5 năm của ngân 
hàng nhằm phản ánh kỳ vọng tích cực đối với việc room ngoại của 
ngân hàng được nới lên 49% theo Nghị định 69/2025/NĐ-CP. 
(2) Phương pháp chiết khấu lợi nhuận thặng dư  
Bên cạnh đó, chúng tôi kết hợp sử dụng thêm phương pháp chiết 
khấu lợi nhuận thặng dư để phản ánh rủi ro hệ thống và kỳ vọng dài 
hạn. 
Khuyến nghị MUA  với cổ phiếu MBB với giá mục tiêu 25,950 VNĐ/CP - tiềm 
năng tăng giá 26.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 17/09/2026.

Index 2026 Q1 = 100 Index 2026 Q1 = 100
Sources: ANZ, Westpac
Despite risk, agricultural export prices to remain elevated.
• Ongoing Middle East tensions are increasing volatility and raising energy, input
and shipping costs, while broadly supporting prices for agricultural products.

Impacts vary depending on idiosyncratic supply and demand conditions.
• Risks are exacerbated by the impact of a now confirmed super-sized El Niño,
which could slice off up to 1ppt of New Zealand’s GDP should on-farm
production of both milk and meat contract significantly.
• We have revised up our farmgate milk price forecast for this season by 25c to
$9.75/kg, with milk powder prices holding up better than we expected. El Niño
presents a clear upside risk to this, as it does not appear to have been reflected

in GDT auction prices to date.
• Meanwhile average New Zealand beef export prices should remain elevated over
the coming year, as lean trimming prices reverse recent declines. Sheepmeat
prices are set to ease on rising Australian supply, but remain at elevated levels.
• Kiwifruit and apple export prices are expected to remain elevated, supported
by strong demand for premium varieties, although increasing supply is likely to
keep prices largely flat rather than drive further gains.

• Export prices for New Zealand logs are set to remain broadly stable to slightly
firmer, supported by lower log supply and balanced Chinese inventories.

Southern Oscillation Index

Output in the agriculture, forestry and fishing

Source: Macrobond, Stats NZ, Westpac
%yr Index
La Niña
El Niño
Farming is well placed to navigate impacts of a strong El Niño.
• Scientists have now confirmed that the current El Niño event will be the

strongest on record. Temperatures in the Pacific Ocean are now 3.05C above the
average, exceeding the record set in 2015.
• A supersized El Niño increases the probability of drier weather in eastern regions
of New Zealand in coming months, with wetter conditions likelier in the west and
southern areas of the country.
• The potential impact on GDP could be significant. Our previous research has
shown a severe drought could slice off as much as 1ppt from economic growth.

• Much of that is will reflect lower agriculture production, particularly in dairy,
sheep and beef farms. Increased on-farm costs and processing disruptions are
also likely.
• A 1% loss in GDP is likely an upper end estimate of the El Niño impact. A key
consideration is soil moisture levels are currently high, implying some resilience
to future drier conditions.
• Farming practices have become more resilient to prolonged dry periods.
Irrigation is more widely used than in past dry periods and snowmelt levels

appear robust, which should be of particular benefit to Canterbury farms.

EL NIÑO

Conflict-related disruptions could push input costs higher than expected.
• Farm input costs are on the rise reflecting two key drivers: fuel and
fertiliser costs.
• Diesel and petrol prices have increased by about 50% compared to the same
period in 2025. While average prices fell in the September quarter, they closed

the quarter at an elevated level as tensions between the US and Iran increased
and broadened to encompass Saudi Arabia and the Yemeni Houthis.
• Fertiliser prices are also significantly higher. While the fertiliser market has
stabilised after a period of extreme volatility earlier this year, prices remain
highly sensitive to developments in the Middle East.
• Nitrogen (urea) prices have fallen substantially from a mid-year spike, with an
pick up in Chinese exports now boosting supply. These prices though remain

vulnerable to supply disruptions.
• By contrast, phosphate and other sulphur-based fertiliser prices are tracking
higher and remain at elevated levels, as supply constraints, trade restrictions
and disruptions to shipping offset the impact of softening demand in some
regions of the world.

Sources: Fonterra, Stats NZ, Westpac Westpac
forecasts
Farmgate milk price forecast revised up on strong protein demand.
• We have revised up our farmgate milk price forecast for this season by 25c to
$9.75/kg. This is above Fonterra’s forecast midpoint of $9.50/kg (range of $8.50-

10.50), but lower than futures market pricing of just over $10/kg.
• We have also opened our forecast for the 2027/28 season at $9.50/kg. Note that
there is a wide margin of uncertainty around the forecast at this stage, and we
will review as the next season approaches.
• Dairy prices at auction have generally held up better than our expectations,
although the performance has varied across products. Trends in global demand
are shifting away from fats and towards proteins, which has boosted demand

and prices for skim milk powder in particular.
• Our forecasts do not include an assumption about the impact of El Niño, which
presents a clear upside risk to prices and a downside risk to volumes. While the
futures market is pricing a strong lift in milk powder prices over the New Zealand
summer, there has been no apparent urgency to buy at the GDT auction.
Fonterra is forecasting a 2% lift in milk volumes over the rest of this season, on
top of what was already a strong increase last season.

• Global milk production also remains ample, although it has slowed in Europe
in recent months. El Niño is less likely to affect conditions among the major
producers in the Northern Hemisphere.

US Beef (Imp 95 CL) prices, 1-month advanced

Source: AgriHQ, Stats NZ
Export beef prices to remain elevated over the next year.
• Beef export prices remain well supported but have softened in recent
months. The weakness has been concentrated in lean beef trimming prices,

which have eased since April as high US prices attracted additional imports,
particularly from South America, while tariff-free quotas temporarily boosted
product availability.
• In contrast, premium beef prices have remained firm, supported by tight global
supplies of high-quality beef, constrained US cattle numbers, and resilient
demand in higher-end retail and foodservice channels.
• We expect that lean trimming prices will reverse recent declines in coming

months as temporary increases in supply begin to fade and underlying
shortages, including the historically small US beef herd, re-emerge.
• In 2027, lean trimming prices should remain elevated but broadly flat as
strong US demand and a small US cattle herd are largely offset by rising global
production. From 2028 onwards, expanding global beef production gradually
eases market conditions, putting pressure on prices.
• Meanwhile, favourable supply/demand fundamentals to support premium beef

prices in 2027, with expanding global production to weigh on prices from 2028.
• Overall, beef export prices are likely to remain high, although both nominal and
real prices are expected to moderate over the medium term.

Source: AgriHQ, Stats NZ
TWI weighted price
Lamb export prices to remain firm, but off recent highs.
• NZ sheepmeat export prices remain near record highs, supported by
constrained global supply and resilient demand from premium markets in

Europe, the UK and North America. Export values rose strongly through 2025/26
despite only modest growth in export volumes, highlighting the strength of
underlying prices.
• Global supply remains tight due to declining sheep flocks and lower slaughter
numbers in New Zealand, the UK and parts of Europe. However, rising Australian
production and exports are expected to gradually lift global supply and increase
competition in key export markets, limiting further price gains.

• Meanwhile, stronger demand from the UK, EU and North America has improved
export returns and reduced New Zealand's reliance on China, where economic
weakness has made consumers more price sensitive and encouraged a shift
towards cheaper proteins and cuts.
• The outlook remains positive but more balanced. Tight supply should keep
prices above historical averages over the coming year, although rising Australian
exports, softer demand growth, affordability pressures, signs of buyer

resistance and (eventually) a stronger NZ dollar could see prices ease modestly
from current elevated levels into 2027.

Capacity is catching up with demand, limiting further price gains.
• NZ kiwifruit export prices have pushed to record highs, supported by strong
demand in North America, Europe, Japan and Korea, as well as continued

consumer preference for premium varieties such as SunGold.
• Export prices continue to edge higher even as a record crop and larger fruit
volumes are helping to reduce market tightness. Zespri notes increased
competition from summer fruit, pricing pressure in parts of Asia and a larger-
than-expected crop.
• Kiwifruit export prices are set to remain high but mostly flat over the next
year, as strong demand for premium varieties such as SunGold and RubyRed is

increasingly offset by rising production and price pressures in Asia.
• Apple export prices are also at record highs, supported by strong demand in
Asia, North America and India, alongside a continued shift towards higher-value
cultivars and premium varieties.
• These factors should keep export prices elevated over the next 12 months,
supporting grower revenues and profitability.
• However, further gains could be limited. Rising New Zealand production,

recovering supplies from key growing regions and larger global apple crops are
expected to increase supply availability, reducing market tightness and keeping
prices broadly stable.

Index IndexLog prices look moribund.
• China remains the dominant market for New Zealand logs, with prices highly
sensitive to construction activity, sawmill demand and daily log offtake. In that
regard, weak housing and furniture demand continue to weigh on prices.

• Chinese softwood log inventories are estimated at around 2.2 to 2.5m m³,
equivalent to roughly 40 to 45 days of inventory cover at current offtake rates.
This suggests inventory levels are broadly balanced and exerting little pressure
on log prices.
• At the same time, reduced New Zealand log supply, including lower harvesting
volumes and the wind-down of salvage logging, is providing some support to
prices. Freight costs also remain an important driver of at-wharf-gate returns,

with currently high shipping costs crimping returns to forest owners.
• NZ log export prices are expected to remain broadly stable to modestly firmer
over the next 12 months, supported by lower domestic supply, balanced Chinese
inventory levels and tentative signs of stabilisation in log demand.
• However, any recovery is likely to be gradual, with weakness in China's property
sector, uncertainty around shipping costs and softer global growth continuing to

constrain demand and cap upside potential.

## Document 361: NZ forecast update, October 2026 (2026-10-01)

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• Higher oil and refined fuels prices, a lower
exchange rate, higher long term interest rates
and weak house price trends justify a review of
our 2026 forecasts.
• We continue to see CPI inflation falling to 3.7%
y/y in the September quarter, but now think
inflation will rise to 4.2% in the December
quarter. Higher global fuel prices and a weaker
New Zealand dollar are driving these trends.
• Commodity export prices remain robust and

are reflected in a rise in our forecast for the
2026/27 Fonterra Milk Price to $9.75/kg. Our
opening bid for the Milk Price in 2027/28 is a
solid $9.50/kg.
• House prices have not stabilised as expected
in recent months. As a result, we have revised
down our 2026 house price forecast from a
small rise to a 1.4% decline.
• The combination of high fuel costs and a weak
housing market will likely crimp consumer
demand running into Christmas.
• We have modestly revised down our GDP

forecasts for the December quarter, which
reduces our 2026 growth forecast to a still
solid 2.5%. Risks appear skewed to the
downside given higher energy prices and long-
term interest rates.
• We will look at our 2027 forecasts in the
coming week.
• Our OCR forecasts remain unchanged. We see
a pause at the October meeting and a hike to
3% at the release of the December Monetary
Policy Statement.
Inflation – All gas, no brakes.
We’ve updated our near-term inflation forecasts in

response to recent global developments.
We continue to estimate that consumer prices rose by
0.7% in the September quarter, consistent with recent
monthly prices updates from Stats NZ. That would see
annual inflation ease to 3.7% in the year to September,
down from 4.1% in the year to June. We’ll confirm that
figure next week, when the final monthly prices update
for the September quarter is released on 16 October.

However, that easing in inflation appears set to be only
a temporary reprieve. We now expect consumer prices
to rise 1.1% in the December quarter. That would see
annual inflation rising to a three-year high of 4.2% by

the end of this year. The main driver of this revision are
sharply higher global fuel prices, compounded by a more
than 4% fall in the New Zealand dollar since the start of
September. Together, those developments have already
pushed local petrol prices up around 50 cents / litre over
the past month, with the nationwide average price of 91
unleaded reaching a record $3.53 / litre earlier this week.

RBNZ target range
Our updated year-end inflation forecast is above the
RBNZ’s September projection of 3.9%. In a recent
presentation, Governor Breman noted that the rise in oil
prices meant the risks to the RBNZ’s near-term inflation
forecast were to the upside. However, she also noted that

the RBNZ’s focus was on the medium-term outlook.
We haven’t changed our quarterly inflation forecasts for
2027 at this stage. However, the upward revision to our
forecast for the December quarter means annual inflation
is set to remain firm through most of 2027, consistent
with the RBNZ’s forecasts. We will review our 2027
forecasts for inflation and growth more comprehensively
next week when we will also review our financial forecasts
with the release of the Westpac Market Outlook.

A more tepid housing market.
We had expected the housing market to stabilise through
the second half of 2026. However, recent trends have
disappointed. House sales volumes continue to weaken,
and the median number of days required to sell a house
has lengthened to 48 days. Not surprisingly, therefore,

average house prices have continued to nudge lower
despite more robust conditions in those areas – mostly
in the South Island – that are most exposed to the well-
performing export sector. The latest indicators suggest
no imminent change in this trend.
Given these weak trends, our previous forecast for
essentially flat house prices through the September and
December quarters now looks too optimistic. We have
revised down our house price forecast for the 2026 year

from a rise of 0.6% y/y to a fall of 1.4% y/y on a national
basis. This is consistent with quarterly falls in house
prices of 0.5% and 0.8% q/q over the September and
December quarters.
The still weak labour market, higher mortgage rates and
still moderate employment growth all point to a weak
housing market for the foreseeable future. Risks remain
tilted towards another weak year in 2027. This would
especially be the case if increased taxation of capital

gains on investor housing were to occur as a result of the
outcome of the 2026 General Election.

It’s likely to be the case that there will be divergent trends
in the housing market across the country. Income and
employment trends in tourism and agriculture dominated

regions are expected to remain robust. Our upgrade to
the 2026/27 Milk Price released in our recent Agri Bites
report emphasises this point. But it looks like it will be
a while before investors and homeowners in the major
metropolitan areas in the North Island will be feeling
wealthier through a rising home value. On balance this
is likely to crimp consumer spending attitudes through
the end of 2026 and might moderate the recovery in the
construction sector that is underway.

Growth forecasts moderated a touch.
Our short-term growth forecasts still appear to be in
the ballpark of what seems likely given recent trends.
Recent consumer and business confidence surveys have

suggested resilience of the economy to recent rises in
energy prices and long-term interest rates. However, it’s
likely the rise in energy prices in particular will eat into
consumer spending appetites towards the end of 2026
should current levels of fuel prices be sustained.
We have knocked off a modest amount off our forecast
for consumer spending in the December quarter to
reflect these risks and will look more closely at the
implications for 2027 growth in coming weeks. Certainly,

the large rise in long term interest rates may be relevant
for 2027 growth trends. The election outcome introduces
two-sided risks to the 2027 growth outlook that are hard
to quantify at this early stage. We currently expect 2.5%
y/y growth for the 2026 year.
We don’t see a need to change our labour market
forecasts for 2026 at this point. Signs continue to
accumulate suggesting strengthening employment
growth – although it will be a while before a noticeable

drop in the unemployment rate is evident.
Implications for the OCR.
We are leaving our forecasts of the OCR unchanged.
Since the July 2026 Monetary Policy Review, we have
anticipated the RBNZ will slow the pace of OCR increases
now the 3% neutral level (in the RBNZ’s view) is coming
into view.
The RBNZ sent a clear signal that they expected to pause
in October and raise the OCR just one more time in
December. We still think this is the most likely scenario.

The recent data has boosted the short-term inflation
profile but at this stage there is little evidence of rising
inflation expectations or more persistent inflationary
pressures. There are some risks to the growth outlook –
albeit two sided – to consider. Taking the time to assess
the impact of the OCR rises to date and the strategy going
forward makes sense to us. We don’t see a shift in the
MPC’s strategy in October.
We continue to think it most likely that the RBNZ will

have more work to do in 2027. However, there is a lot
of water to go under the bridge before then. The OCR
increases that have occurred to date, and the one further
expected increase in December will position the MPC
well to respond to conditions in 2027 as they unfold.
The unexpected rise in long term interest rates that
has occurred in the last 6 weeks or so has tightened
monetary conditions and has at least offset the easing in
conditions coming from the weaker exchange rate over

the same period.

## Document 388: The Institute Employment Report_ September 2026 Bank of America Institute analysis shows slower payroll growth, declining unemployment payments and continued strength in lower-inco

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Institute Employment Report_ September 2026 Bank of America Institute analysis shows slower payroll growth, declining unemployment payments and continued strength in lower-inco_223e3a6a.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13025075     1

Economy 
The Institute Employment Report: September 2026 
07 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Job growth softened further in September, according to Bank of America's deposit data, with growth of 1.4% year-over-year 
(YoY), from 1.5% in August. But falling unemployment payments in September indicate a relatively healthy overall picture. 
• Lower-income households' wage growth remains strongest, despite a narrowing gap. After-tax wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY 
for lower-income households in September, while rising to 3.8% and 3.7% for middle- and higher-income households, 
respectively. 
• Lower tax withholdings and a relative strengthening in the younger/lower-income end of the labor market are driving this story. 
Additionally, average hours have risen in some lower-paid industries, which is also boosting pay packets.

Job growth slowed in September, but unemployment trends are benign  
Our estimate of job growth – which uses Bank of America deposit data to track changes in the number of customer accounts 
receiving a paycheck (see Methodology) – slowed further in September, to 1.4% year-over-year (YoY) from 1.5% YoY in August. 
The data can be noisy, partly due to seasonal variation and differences in pay-period timing, but in our view, the latest reading is 
consistent with the easing in payrolls growth seen in the September Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) data (Exhibit 1). 
Still, despite slower hiring, there is little sign of a rise in joblessness in Bank of America data. Unemployment payments into 
customer accounts showed a YoY decline in September (Exhibit 2). So, overall, labor market trends appear resilient.

After-tax wage growth cooled for lower-income, but rose for other cohorts 
Lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY in September from 4.7% YoY in August, while wage growth for middle- and 
higher-income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. Overall, these changes were modest, and lower-income 
households are continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” 
pattern we observed over much of 2025 and 2026 (Exhibit 3). 
Exhibit 3: Lower-income wage growth remains strongest, despite a narrowing gap 
After-tax wage and salary growth by household income terciles, based on Bank of America aggregated consumer deposit account data (three-month moving 
average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
In our view, the pick-up in lower- and middle-income households’ after-tax wage growth continues to reflect the impact of the 
One Big Beautiful Bill Act (OBBA) in reducing tax withholdings, alongside an underlying rise in job-to-job changes at the 
younger/lower-income end of the labor market (for more, read: The Institute Employment Report: August 2026). Consistent with 
that underlying pick up, BLS data shows a solid YoY rise in average hours worked in several relatively lower-paid industries, such 
as manufacturing, retail and other services. Hourly workers in these industries are likely seeing rising paychecks as a result 
(Exhibit 4).

I NSTI TUTE

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Estimate of payroll growth from Bank of America internal data is based on the change in customer accounts receiving a paycheck in the month. 
An adjustment is made for the difference between overall population growth and customer account growth. Occasionally historic payments can 
be reclassified, leading to revisions in the series.  
An estimate of bonus growth from Bank of America deposit data is calculated by looking at customers who have received an inbound ACH 
payroll transaction in the last two years. From this sample an estimate of bonuses is derived by looking for payroll transactions which are over 
50% higher than the median regular payroll payments received by the customer. Of these payments only those that were received around the 
same time in each of the last two years are selected.  
The job-to-job change rate (j2j rate) is defined as the proportion of customers with an identified change in their employer as a proportion of 
the total number of customers with employment income. We estimate the median pay rise associated with a j2j change using the pay in the 
first three months of the new job compared to the same three months a year ago. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Diplomacy appears firmly back in play. The past month saw continued fighting and renewed diplomacy.
Reporting suggests that the US is prepared to extend troop deployments in the Middle East to 2027. Iran
targeted both US forces in the region and some tankers moving through the Strait, but has been unable to
prevent a significant rebound in crude flows through Hormuz. On 22 SEP, talks were mediated by Qatar,

Pakistan and Egypt, and on 24 SEP, Iran presented the US with a seven-day roadmap to a ceasefire leading
to a phased reopening of the Strait of Hormuz and a return to nuclear negotiations, conditional on a US
lifting of its blockade of Iran, sanctions relief and broader cessation of hostilities. The Trump
Administration rejected the proposal, but negotiations continue.
To watch will be whether October is the month the war transitions into a negotiated settlement, which

may hinge on whether Iran is willing to make some concessions on sanctions relief and the nuclear
question, and whether Trump is keen to demonstrate that the war has produced a political and economic
result in the final weeks before the 3 NOV midterm elections. It is plausible that the current diplomatic
channel could produce a temporary de-escalation mechanism as long as there is pathway around the
broader nuclear issue.
Both sides will reconvene for trade talks in Beijing on 8-9 October, which the EU views as a deadline for

“tangible results”. Brussels remains focused on its rising trade deficit with China – which has reached a
record €1 billion per day – and the impact of Chinese goods on European industry. The bloc has vowed to
take measures if talks fail.
While both sides could achieve progress in talks, a lasting breakthrough appears improbable, setting the
stage for more trade tensions into 2027. New EU trade investigations, curbs on Chinese imports, and EU-
level instruments intended to address alleged Chinese overcapacity, are possible outcomes, to which

Beijing has stated it would respond.
China-EU trade talks could also impact China-US relations. Should talks with Brussels fall short of averting
increased European trade pressure, we assess that Beijing may further prioritise near-term US stability. See
Rules of Engagement: Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties for more.

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026

Latvia will elect all 100 members of the 15th Saeima. Latvia, which borders Russia and Belarus, has been
among the most vocal supporters of Ukraine. The next government will set the country’s course on
defence spending, Ukraine aid, sanctions enforcement and border security. The field is highly fragmented
with the governing parties lacking a majority in the polls. The fragmentation matters as a weakened and

multi-party scramble could mean a slower, more unstable coalition.
Brazil’s first electoral round is on 4 OCT, with a runoff on 25 OCT. This is a critical election as it will
decide whether Latin America’s biggest economy continues President Lula’s state-led social spending or
pivots to a more fiscally-orthodox agenda under Bolsonaro. Polls have gone from a Lula lead in the spring
to a statistical dead heat between Lula and Bolsonaro. A Brazilian Supreme Court crisis – which has

implicated Supreme Court Justices and politicians on both sides – is weighing on the race. Congressional
and gubernatorial races will also impact the governability of the country.
This election will also shape whether the country hedges between Washington and Beijing under Lula or
tilts toward closer US trade and security alignment under Bolsonaro with potential implications for
tariffs, BRICS+ cohesion, and Latin America’s broader positioning between the two powers.

The meetings will take place in Bangkok, bringing finance ministers, central bankers, policy-makers and
business leaders together. The agenda is expected to centre on global growth, financial stability, debt,
trade and investment, development finance and the impact of AI on jobs and productivity, with particular
attention given to mobilizing private capital. The key watchpoint will be the outlook for global growth
and inflation, debt vulnerabilities, the future of the international financial architecture and how policy-

makers respond to increasing fragmentation in trade and capital flows.

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026

Leaders are expected to focus heavily on Ukraine, European security and defence, competitiveness and
the EU’s economic position in a more contested global environment. They will also take forward
negotiations on the 2028-2034 EU long-term budget. They set themselves a soft deadline in June to get
the budget broadly locked down because missing it pushes ratification into 2027 and risks a funding gap

for beneficiaries from JAN 2028. To watch will be a genuine breakthrough on the multiannual framework
or more likely what “substantial” progress may look like. Ukraine funding is the other pressure point. This
meeting will be important for understanding the direction of European defence spending and industrial
policy, the next phase of budget negotiations and the EU response to growing external economic
pressure and strategic dependencies.
The Canadian province of Alberta will hold a referendum, which includes a question about the province’s

future within Canada. This is not an independence vote itself but asking voters to choose between
remaining a Canadian province and asking the Alberta government to begin the constitutional process
required for a future binding referendum on separation. Current polling suggests “remain” is comfortably
ahead. The significance is around potential renewed uncertainty around Canada’s federal cohesion,
energy policy, fiscal transfers and Alberta's relationship with Ottawa and Washington. Even a modest

agreement to begin a process towards secession would likely keep alive equalization grievances as a
structural fault line in Canadian federalism feeding into political risk.
.
The event’s theme focuses on “Advancing Alliances in a New Era.” It will tackle how governments and
businesses respond to a world where geopolitics, economic security, supply chains and technology are
increasingly intertwined. There is likely to be a strong focus on the future of the transatlantic

relationship, including how Europe adapts to a more demanding security environment. There will also be
discussions of tariffs, supply chain resilience, critical capabilities and the weaponization of economic
interdependence. It will align with Chancellor Merz’s efforts to ensure Europe actively shapes outcomes
around strategic autonomy and reducing China exposure in manufacturing.

The second summit will take place in Saudi Arabi, bringing EU and Gulf leaders together. The agenda is
expected to focus on regional security and the Middle East, energy and economic security, trade and

particularly salient given the current crisis in the region. Economically, the relationship is substantial: EU-

GCC trade reached €165.6bn in 2025.
To watch will be movement on the long-stalled EU-GCC free trade talks and visa-waiver requests from
Qatar and Saudi Arabia in addition to the extent of the focus on Gaza reconstruction, Iran and regional
security get relative to the economic agenda.
GCC states have consistently used these summits to press for clearer EU positioning on Middle East

changes and personnel reshuffles, institutional reforms, and political priorities for the next Congress.

Five-Year Plan (2026–2030) were already adopted during the Fourth Plenum in OCT 2025, allowing the
upcoming session to focus on personnel and governance matters.

Elections will be held for the 26th Knesset, the first since the October 7th attacks and wars in Gaza,

in Gaza factor into campaign messaging, and whether a Netanyahu-led bloc can reassemble a working

outcome will shape Israel’s posture on critical regional issues, including Gaza reconstruction, Iran policy
and West Bank settlement activity.
This summit is shaping up as a particularly important juncture against a more ambitious push to redefine
the relationship. During Mark Carney’s SEP visit to Strasbourg, he called for a comprehensive strategic
alliance with Europe. European Commission President von der Leyen went further, proposing that Canada

become the EU’s first “associate member”.
The agenda is expected to centre on trade and economic security, defense, critical minerals, energy,
digital cooperation and supply-chain resilience, building on the EU-Canada security and Defense
Partnership in the EU’s SAFE defense initiative. The summit will be watched for concrete progress on a
digital trade agreement and broader economic cooperation.

## Document 403: TPCA 2026 quick takeaways - 大尺寸、高層數、整合多顆內埋Bridge _ 矽電容、多層芯板

Source PDF: all report pdf/TPCA 2026 quick takeaways - 大尺寸、高層數、整合多顆內埋Bridge _ 矽電容、多層芯板.pdf
Report source: line報告備份

### Page 1

台灣股市｜科技 
事件分析

Fubon Research 
2026 年 10 月 20 日

資料來源: TPCA

黃韻庭 
yunting.huang@fubon.com
